Native, because by then the GL context and imgui are gone or untrustworthy; the folder rather than the file, because the log's name is not something to have to know; through the shell and outside the kill-on-close job, for `Ctx.open_log`'s reason. It runs **after teardown**, which is why `run` carries a flag rather than reporting from the `except`: the journal's last write goes through the task runner, and the sentence it prints counts what is on disk. That sentence is computed rather than promised — "your work is safe" is only worth saying when it is true.

**The supported runtime is checkout-shaped, and it is checked.** `config.source_checkout()` asks whether `pyproject.toml` sits beside the package's parent, and `studio.main.run` refuses with a native dialog when it does not. A source clone and the Windows installer both provide that same root shape (`src/warlock`, `vendor`, `docs/manual`, and `pyproject.toml`); a bare wheel still does not. Every native default therefore continues to resolve against `PROJECT_ROOT` (`__file__.parents[2]`) with no installed-only path branch. The installer stages only the runtime and never model weights. `installer/runtime-manifest.json` pins every ignored vendor executable and DLL by safe relative path, byte size and SHA-256; `verify_runtime.py` checks the checkout and staged copy and rejects unlisted files, while `install.json` records the complete manifest's digest. **A fetch carries its pin, and every entry has one.** `models.Fetch.revision` travels registry → `fetch.Job` → the worker's `snapshot_download(revision=...)` → the rendered paste-able command, so a hand-run download cannot silently take the branch tip. It is a 40-hex commit on all 21 records over 20 repositories, asserted rather than hoped (`tests/test_fetch.py::test_every_fetch_pins_an_immutable_commit`) — the field's own docstring had promised that test since the day it was added and every value was `""`. The pins were recovered offline from the `.metadata` sidecars beside the already-downloaded files, so each is the commit *this* host runs; the one that could not be checked against bytes carries a comment saying so. `""` still means *not pinned yet* and still says so; it does not mean "main" dressed as a version. **`fetch._merge` carries it too**, which it did not: `Job` defaults `revision` to `""` and the merge rebuilt the job from scratch, so the IP-Adapter — the one repository with two records against one destination — came out of `plan` unpinned while `models.py` and the printed command both showed the pin, silently, because an unpinned fetch succeeds. Two records naming one repository must *agree* about the commit and a disagreement raises: a directory cannot hold two snapshots. The documented commands are pinned to the same SHAs and a test compares the two, because four pins already in `models.py` were invisible in `docs/MODELS.md` for months.

**A download is verified before it is published, and can be verified again afterwards.** Two checks against two different references, and neither finds what the other does. The worker re-hashes the *staged* tree against the ETags the hub recorded per file (`hashes.expected_digests`, off `snapshot_download`'s `.cache/huggingface/download/*.metadata`) before the rename, before that cache is deleted and before anything moves — mandatory, because a file that reaches a model directory wrong reads as a finished model forever. **Two digest shapes, and the difference is a trap**: an LFS ETag *is* the content's SHA-256 (every weight file here), while a small non-LFS one is the **git blob SHA-1** — `sha1(b"blob <len>\0" + content)`, not a hash of the bytes — so a verifier comparing a plain SHA-1 would report every `config.json` in the registry as corrupt, which is the failure that teaches a user to ignore the check. A file with no sidecar is skipped, never failed: "nothing to compare against" is not evidence of corruption. The publish then records `{path: {size, sha256}}` into `.warlock-fetch.json`, and `fetch.verify_manifest` re-reads that **on demand only** — `warlock doctor --verify`, never at startup (the C29/C30 lesson: hashing 16 GB is not something opening the app asks for). A manifest with no digests verifies **`unknown`** and is never red, because every directory installed before this and every hand-run `hf download` is one. It stays out of `fetch.present`, which is called from the frame loop and from the admission doors — and which the one-byte fixtures depend on.

**A selection of models is one transaction.** MDL-10 closed. Repositories used to publish one at a time, so a failure on the third of four left two installed that nobody had asked for individually — and the refusal said so, because saying otherwise would have been a lie. The children stage only now (`publish: False`; they stop with the tree downloaded, verified and marked by `.warlock-publish.json`, which is what distinguishes "finished, waiting" from "interrupted"), and the parent publishes all of them afterwards under `_maintenance`. A failure during staging installs nothing because nothing moved. The publishing phase is covered by `<model_root>/.warlock-txn.json`, written before the first file moves and updated *per destination as it happens* — the crash it survives happens during the loop, so the journal has to be true at every moment of it rather than only at the ends. `downloads.recover` reads it on the next launch, from `Runtime._start` after `reconcile_startup()` and **before** the staging sweep; the sweep spares any tree an open journal names, or it would delete the only copy of the files the rollback was about to restore. It rolls **back**, never forward: the one thing known about a process that died mid-write is that its last write may be torn. `warlock/publish.py` owns the move, the undo and the journal, stdlib-only, because the parent and the child are two processes and two copies of a rollback are two rollbacks.

**Nothing executes Python it downloaded.** BiRefNet's modelling code is vendored at `src/warlock/pipelines/birefnet/` and `trust_remote_code` appears in no call anywhere in `src/` (AST-scanned, not grepped — the phrase is in half a dozen comments explaining why it is gone). `local_files_only=True` and a pinned revision had already narrowed the risk to "whatever that commit shipped"; what neither fixes is that `uv.lock` says nothing about runtime-downloaded code and no reviewer of this repository could read what the process would run. `*.py` is dropped from the download too — Python in a model directory that nothing runs is worse than either extreme, because a reader finding it would assume it *is* what runs. `ATTRIBUTION.md` beside the source carries the upstream commit, the licence, the originals' SHA-256s and the **four** deliberate modifications, each marked `WARLOCK n/4` in the file: the torchvision weights-enum download paths deleted, and three `eval()`-on-a-config-string dispatches replaced by tables. Nothing else is touched and ruff does not lint it — a reformatted vendor copy is one nobody can diff against upstream, which is the entire reason it is in the tree. `birefnet.load` uses **`load_state_dict(strict=True)`**, which is a parity assertion rather than a setting: the loose load `from_pretrained` performs would leave a layer randomly initialised if the source and the checkpoint drifted, and a BiRefNet with one random decoder block does not fail — it returns a plausible, wrong mask. The mask was captured through the old path before the switch and the vendored one reproduces it **bit for bit**, 0 of 65,536 pixels differing (`tests/test_birefnet_parity.py`, `gpu`-marked because it needs the real checkpoint rather than a card).

`fetch.suspect_files` answers the *second* question presence never did: a zero-length safetensors passes every `Path.exists()` and fails minutes later with the checkpoint already resident. It is the cheap probe — one `stat` per file — that runs where a probe belongs, in front of the expensive verification above. Test fixtures therefore write one byte, not zero — an empty placeholder now means "corrupt". **`scripts/preflight.py` is the release gate.** Version lockstep across `pyproject.toml`, `__version__` and the CHANGELOG heading, then ruff, then the non-GPU suite, then a line saying the GPU lane did *not* run. Two correct tests existed for REL-01 and shipped it anyway because nobody ran them; a gate that lives where the release does not happen is a gate nobody passes through.

**A skeleton is a JSON template, not code.** `src/warlock/templates/*.json` hold normalized landmark joints in a unit bbox (Blender axes: +X the subject's left, −Y forward, +Z up; x/y span −0.5..0.5, z spans 0..1). `rigging.fit_template` scales them onto the measured mesh bbox. Add a skeleton by adding a file, never by hardcoding bones in `blender_worker.py`. Fitting is bbox-proportional and deliberately approximate — the fitted positions are recorded in `rig.json` so a later "adjust joints" pass can correct them without re-rigging.
- Rigging is the `rig` extra (`uv sync --extra rig`). bpy ships **cp313 wheels only**, so the requirement carries a `python_version` marker — without it the whole project becomes unresolvable on 3.12 rather than just losing rigging. Missing bpy is non-fatal everywhere: `doctor.blender_check` reports it and the UI hides the rig controls.
- **`src/warlock/bench/` is the package; `~/.warlock/bench/` is its output directory** — two different things one word away from each other, so both are written out in full below and wherever else the distinction matters (`bench/findings.json`, `bench/runs/`, `bench/calibrate/` are *outputs*). The package is the *regression* half only — the fixed suite, the recipes, the manifest, `score`/`calibrate`/`views`. The parameter-sweep half (`src/warlock/bench/sweep.py`, `bench/verdicts.py`, `bench/report.py`, `src/warlock/bench/sweeps/`, all deleted in `1fc1573`, v0.0.8) is gone: it stood up its own `Runtime`, which collides with a running app. Two live modules sit one word away from those names and must not be swept up with them: `src/warlock/sweep.py` is the trellis `--band` sweep, CLI-wired (`warlock sweep`) and tested, and `src/warlock/service/verdicts.py` is the verdict table's service half. `src/warlock/bench/findings.py` stays as the pure-stdlib *reader* the panes import; `src/warlock/service/findings.py` is what writes the file into the `~/.warlock/bench/` directory.
- `docs/` holds six kinds of thing: this invariants reference, the manual (`docs/manual/`, which `tests/manual/test_docs.py` validates), the measurement documents under `docs/measurements/` (a dozen now, and the count is the wrong thing to track — the rule is what matters: a constant the stored corpus is keyed on gets one *before* it changes, and so does a performance claim, which is why the 2026-08-20 stroke-invalidation document records the rejected first attempt as well as the landed one), the optional-model catalogue `docs/MODELS.md`, and `docs/COMPAT.md`, which holds **both** interop ledgers — Plotter↔Tiled and Inker↔Aseprite, merged into one file on 2026-08-21 from `docs/PLOTTER_COMPAT.md` and `docs/ASEPRITE_INTEROP.md`, both now deleted. **Only one of its two parts is executable, and the scoping is an invariant.** `tests/plotter/test_compat_matrix.py` parses the Tiled part's tables as data, so six tests fail if a row is edited carelessly; its `_CHECKED_PART` guard stops at the Aseprite heading, because that part's rows are spelled identically over an entirely different state vocabulary (`dropped`, `warned`, `n/a on write`) and a widened scan would report them as Tiled rows in unknown states — whose obvious "fix" is to merge the two vocabularies, at which point neither ledger says what it meant. `test_the_scan_stops_at_the_aseprite_part` asserts the boundary rather than trusting it. The Aseprite part stays prose: there is no `.aseprite` corpus-as-data test, only the fixed-point gate `tests/inker/test_aseprite_corpus.py` beside it. The ComfyUI research note `docs/REPORT.md` used to be one of these and was deleted in `09c64b4`, and that this line went two waves without noticing is itself instructive — the link checker skipped it, because `DEAD_CITE_MARKER` exempts any line containing the word "deleted" and this one always had it. A constant the stored corpus is keyed on gets a measurement document before it changes — that is the pattern `trellis_band`, `mesh_hole_max` and `SEAM_MAX` all set. **There is one plan file and it is `TODO.md` at the root, and everything about it is a constraint.** It was written on 2026-08-21 by consolidating the four that had accumulated — `MY_TODO.md`, `LPC_ALT.md` (the Troupe programme), `EXE_PLAN.md` (the installer) and the user-owed passes out of the two interop ledgers — and it holds exactly two kinds of entry: **what a human has to do** (art direction, authoring keyframes, opening a file in real Aseprite or real Tiled, running the GPU lane, and the design decisions that are genuinely the user's), and **work that is fully specified and deliberately unstarted**, which today is the installer and Troupe's open phases. Neither is derivable from the tree. The moment an item could be built it is built and struck out rather than tracked, and a *record* of shipped work does not go there — `LPC_ALT.md` was ~85% that, and what survived it is in this file and as passing oracles in `studio/troupe/ulpc.py`. A plan whose boxes disagree with the tree is worse than no plan. **The filename was reclaimed deliberately, and the citations that made it dangerous are gone.** `docs/TODO.md` — the UX/UI review report that replaced `docs/LEFTOVERS.md` on 2026-08-10 — was deleted on 2026-08-11 in `de87838`, and for ten months a bare `TODO.md §N` citation was defined to resolve against `docs/LEFTOVERS.md`, a *different* deleted file, which is why recreating the name was forbidden. All fourteen were rewritten on 2026-08-21 to name the programme they meant (the quality judge, tier qualification) or the `docs/measurements/` document that records the reasoning, so **no `§N` API exists anywhere in the tree and none is to be minted**. `tests/test_ux_todo_fixes.py::test_no_module_cites_the_plan_file` is the enforcement, widened from `studio/` to all of `src` and `scripts` in the same change now that no grandfathered site remains: a plan file is not a citable reference. What a module needs to explain, it explains where it is, or here, or in a measurement document. Git keeps every deleted plan and `git log --diff-filter=D` finds them. Every roadmap, plan and survey file that used to sit beside it — `SUGGESTIONS.md`, `CLAY.md`, `NEXT.md`, `CLAY_ELEMENTS.md`, `QA_AUDIT.md`, `docs/JUDGE.md`, `ANALYSIS.md`, the `superpowers/plans` and `superpowers/specs` trees, and, on 2026-08-08, `TODO.md`, `NEXT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/LIST.md` and `docs/BUILD_PLAN.md` — was executed or superseded and has been deleted; `git log --diff-filter=D` finds them if an old reference needs chasing. **On 2026-08-15 the last three went the same way**: `REDESIGN.md` (the six-wave UI programme, all six waves merged by `bf8889c`, whose outcome this file already carries), `INKER_UPDATE.md` (the Aseprite-parity plan, merged at `28e35d9`, whose one section worth keeping — the eighteen deliberate divergences — was folded into the Inker block above rather than deleted with it), and `NEXT_SESSION.md` (which opened by calling itself a record and not a queue). Their eighty-odd `REDESIGN.md wave N` citations were rewritten to name the *programme* rather than the file — "the UI redesign, wave N" — because a wave's outcome is described here, so the rewrite points at a live record. The `TODO.md §N` citations were the one case held back from that treatment, on the grounds that they pointed at reasoning with no other home; they were rewritten the same way on 2026-08-21 when the filename was reclaimed, and the grounds turned out to be false — every one of them meant either the quality-judge programme or tier qualification, both of which have measurement documents. `tests/test_ux_todo_fixes.py` sweeps every retired plan filename out of `src/` and `scripts/` so none can drift back in. **On 2026-08-20 two more went, for the same reason and by the same procedure**: `ASEPRITE_PARITY.md` (the six-wave parity programme, every wave and every one of their owed items closed — its non-goals, its P1 backlog and its rule of engagement folded into this file, its citations rewritten to name the programme, and two of those citations fixed on the way because they had always named the wrong file for divergence 19) and `UPDATE_2.md` (seventeen parts, all done, and — unusually — not one citation anywhere in the tree, so it went with nothing to rewrite). Both are in `RETIRED_PLANS` now. The rule they died under is that a plan whose boxes disagree with the tree is worse than no plan, so a finished one is deleted rather than ticked: git keeps it, and the code is the record. Five more went on 2026-08-21, consolidated into `TODO.md`: `LPC_ALT.md`, `EXE_PLAN.md` and `MY_TODO.md`, plus the two interop ledgers, which merged rather than died. Two consequences of the 2026-08-08 consolidation used to be load-bearing and one of them no longer is. The deleted `docs/LEFTOVERS.md` **preserved the old `TODO.md`'s `§` numbering**, and until 2026-08-21 fourteen source, test and script files cited `TODO.md §2`/`§3`/`§5`/`§7`/`§8`/`§10` by number, meaning that file's §N and never the `docs/TODO.md` that replaced it. **They are all rewritten now** and the numbering is dead as an API — if an old commit's citation needs chasing, `git log --diff-filter=D` still finds the file, but nothing live points at it. And LEFTOVERS' closing appendix kept the handful of things a diff could not carry — the build waves' file-contention map, the three departures the judge made from its own written design, and the fact that the smoke suite runs at UI scale 1.0 and nothing else does — because deleting a plan was never meant to delete the reasoning; that appendix is likewise read out of git history now.

**The keyboard-remap exception in the Aseprite P1 record is retired.** Commands, tools, quick tools and contextual action modifiers are separate `inker_ops.Binding` records; target-scoped overrides replace only the changed default group, an empty group means deliberately unbound, and override priority wins a collision. Menus, tooltips, event dispatch, the searchable editor and `inker_ops.manifest()` consume that registry. Overrides are validated, versioned JSON deltas in the Inker settings block, so untouched defaults can improve without rewriting a user's choices; import/export uses the same deterministic spelling. `Op.key` survives only as the compatibility view used to derive each command's primary default, not as the dispatch model.

See memory (`warlock-stack.md`) for more background on this stack.
